 133: struct pcc2_data {
 134: 	struct interrupt	cpu_irq;
 135: 
 136: 	uint8_t			pcctwo_reg[PCC2_SIZE];
 137: 
 138: 	/*  pcc2widthkills: one bit per width-guard block, see the enum.  Zeroed by
 139: 	    the init memset, so every instance starts with every announce armed.  */
 140: 	uint32_t		warned;
 141: 	/*  #501: one bit per BYTE OFFSET (0..0x3f) for the default: arm's announce;
 142: 	    relative_addr is folded to PCC2_SIZE above the switch, so the shift is
 143: 	    always defined.  Zeroed by the same memset.  */
 144: 	uint64_t		warned_unimpl;
 145: 
 146: 	uint8_t			cur_int_vec[8];
 147: 
 148: 	/*  Timers:  */
 149: 	struct timer		*timer;
 150: 	int			pending_timer1_interrupts;
 151: 	int			pending_timer2_interrupts;
 152: };
 153: /*  pcc2widthkills: the enum must fit the field.  A seat pointed out that a bare
 154:     `<= 32' tested a literal, not the field, so narrowing `warned' would compile
 155:     silently; this is dev_dreamcast_gdrom.c's own form.  Negative array size is a
 156:     compile-time error, C99, no assert.h.  */
 157: typedef char pcc2_w_fits_in_warned[(PCC2_W_NBITS <= 8 * sizeof(((struct pcc2_data *)0)->warned)
 158:     && PCC2_W_NBITS <= 8 * sizeof(1u)) ? 1 : -1];
 159: 
 160: 
 161: static uint32_t read32(unsigned char *p)
 162: {
 163: 	uint32_t x = 0;
 164: 	size_t i;
 165: 
 166: 	for (i=0; i<sizeof(x); i++) {
 167: 		x <<= 8;
 168: 		x |= p[i];
 169: 	}
 170: 
 171: 	return x;
 172: }
 173: 
 174: 
 175: static void write32(unsigned char *p, uint32_t x)
 176: {
 177: 	size_t i;
 178: 
 179: 	for (i=0; i<sizeof(x); i++) {
 180: 		p[sizeof(x) - 1 - i] = x;
 181: 		x >>= 8;
 182: 	}
 183: }
 184: 
 185: 
 186: static void pcc_timer_tick(struct timer *t, void *extra)
 187: {
 188: 	struct pcc2_data *d = (struct pcc2_data *) extra;
 189: 
 190: 	/*  The PCC2 timers run at 1 MHz.  */
 191: 	int steps = (int) (1000000.0 / PCC_TIMER_TICK_HZ);
 192: 
 193: 	uint32_t count1, count2, compare1, compare2;
 194: 	int interrupt1 = 0, interrupt2 = 0;
 195: 
 196: 	/*  Read values:  */
 197: 	count1 = read32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1COUNT]);
 198: 	count2 = read32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2COUNT]);
 199: 	compare1 = read32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CMP]);
 200: 	compare2 = read32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CMP]);
 201: 
 202: 	/*  Timer enabled? Then count...  */
 203: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] & PCC2_TCTL_CEN) {
 204: 		uint32_t old_count1 = count1;
 205: 		count1 += steps;
 206: 
 207: 		/*  Did we pass the compare value?  */
 208: 		if ((old_count1 < compare1 && count1 >= compare1) ||
 209: 		    (old_count1 < compare1 && count1 < old_count1)) {
 210: 			interrupt1 = 1;
 211: 
 212: 			if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] & PCC2_TCTL_COC) {
 213: 				while (count1 >= compare1) {
 214: 					count1 -= compare1;
 215: 					interrupt1 ++;
 216: 				}
 217: 				interrupt1 --;
 218: 			}
 219: 		}
 220: 
 221: 		/*  Overflow?  */
 222: 		if ((int32_t) old_count1 >= 0 && (int32_t) count1 < 0) {
 223: 			int oc = 1 + (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] >> 4);
 224: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] &= ~PCC2_TCTL_OVF;
 225: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] |= (oc << 4);
 226: 		}
 227: 	}
 228: 
 229: 	/*  ... and the same for timer 2:  */
 230: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] & PCC2_TCTL_CEN) {
 231: 		uint32_t old_count2 = count2;
 232: 		count2 += steps;
 233: 
 234: 		if ((old_count2 < compare2 && count2 >= compare2) ||
 235: 		    (old_count2 < compare2 && count2 < old_count2)) {
 236: 			interrupt2 = 1;
 237: 			if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] & PCC2_TCTL_COC) {
 238: 				while (count2 >= compare2) {
 239: 					count2 -= compare2;
 240: 					interrupt2 ++;
 241: 				}
 242: 				interrupt2 --;
 243: 			}
 244: 		}
 245: 
 246: 		if ((int32_t) old_count2 >= 0 && (int32_t) count2 < 0) {
 247: 			int oc = 1 + (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] >> 4);
 248: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] &= ~PCC2_TCTL_OVF;
 249: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] |= (oc << 4);
 250: 		}
 251: 	}
 252: 
 253: 	/*  Should we cause interrupts?  */
 254: 	if (interrupt1 && d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IEN)
 255: 		d->pending_timer1_interrupts += interrupt1;
 256: 	if (interrupt2 && d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IEN)
 257: 		d->pending_timer2_interrupts += interrupt2;
 258: 
 259: 	/*  Write back values:  */
 260: 	write32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1COUNT], count1);
 261: 	write32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2COUNT], count2);
 262: }
 263: 
 264: 
 265: static void reassert_interrupts(struct pcc2_data *d)
 266: {
 267: 	int assert = 0;
 268: 
 269: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_GENCTL] & PCC2_C040) {
 270: 		/*  The M68000 interrupt mechanism involves outputting
 271: 		    interrupt level on pins EIPL<2..0>. Not implemented
 272: 		    yet.  */
 273: 		fatal("pcc2: C040 interrupt assertions... TODO\n");
 274: 		exit(1);
 275: 	}
 276: 
 277: 	/*
 278: 	 *  Recalculate current interrupt level:
 279: 	 */
 280: 	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = 0;
 281: 
 282: 	/*  Timer interrupts?  */
 283: 	if (d->pending_timer1_interrupts > 0 &&
 284: 	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IEN)
 285: 		d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] |= PCC2_TTIRQ_INT;
 286: 	if (d->pending_timer2_interrupts > 0 &&
 287: 	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IEN)
 288: 		d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] |= PCC2_TTIRQ_INT;
 289: 
 290: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_INT) {
 291: 		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IL;
 292: 		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_TIMER1;
 293: 		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
 294: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
 295: 	}
 296: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_INT) {
 297: 		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IL;
 298: 		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_TIMER2;
 299: 		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
 300: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
 301: 	}
 302: 
 303: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCTX] & PCC2_IRQ_IEN &&
 304: 	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCTX] & PCC2_IRQ_INT) {
 305: 		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCTX] & PCC2_IRQ_IPL;
 306: 		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_SCC_TX;
 307: 		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
 308: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
 309: 	}
 310: 
 311: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCRX] & PCC2_IRQ_IEN &&
 312: 	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCRX] & PCC2_IRQ_INT) {
 313: 		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCRX] & PCC2_IRQ_IPL;
 314: 		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_SCC_RX;
 315: 		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
 316: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
 317: 	}
 318: 
 319: 	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCSIICR] & PCC2_IRQ_IEN &&
 320: 	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCSIICR] & PCC2_IRQ_INT) {
 321: 		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCSIICR] & PCC2_IRQ_IPL;
 322: 		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_SCSI;
 323: 		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
 324: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
 325: 	}
 326: 
 327: 	/*  TODO: Other interrupt sources.  */
 328: 
 329: 	/*  Assert interrupt on the CPU if the IPL is higher than the mask:  */
 330: 	if ((d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] & INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK) >
 331: 	    (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_MASK] & INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK))
 332: 		assert = 1;
 333: 
 334: 	debug("[ pcc2: IPL = %i, MASK = %i => %s ]\n", d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL]& INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK,
 335: 		d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_MASK] & INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK,
 655: 			pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_MASK_LEN, "PCCTWO_MASK", len);
 656: 			break;
 657: 		}
 658: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 659: 			d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] = idata;
 660: 			reassert_interrupts(d);
 661: 		} 720:  733: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct pcc2_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct pcc2_data)));
 734: 	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct pcc2_data));
 735: 
 736: 	/*
 737: 	 *  Initial values, according to the manual:
 738: 	 *
 739: 	 *  VECBASE is 0x0f after a reset, although the lowest four bits
 740: 	 *  cannot be manually written to after startup.
 741: 	 */
 742: 	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_CHIPID] = PCC2_ID;
 743: 	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_CHIPREV] = 0x00;
 744: 	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_GENCTL] = 0x00;
 745: 	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_VECBASE] = 0x0f;
 746: 	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_PSCALEADJ] = 256 - 33;	// 256 - MHz (fake)
 747: 
 748: 	/*  Connect to the CPU's interrupt pin:  */
 749: 	INTERRUPT_CONNECT(devinit->interrupt_path, d->cpu_irq);
 750: 
 751: 	/*
 752: 	 *  Register the 16 PCC2 interrupt vectors:
 753: 	 */
 754: 	for (i=0; i<16; i++) {
 755: 		struct interrupt templ;
 756: 		char n[300];
 757: 		snprintf(n, sizeof(n), "%s.pcc2.%i",
 758: 		    devinit->interrupt_path, i);
 759: 
 760: 		memset(&templ, 0, sizeof(templ));
 761: 		templ.line = i;
 762: 		templ.name = n;
 763: 		templ.extra = d;
 764: 		templ.interrupt_assert = pcctwo_interrupt_assert;
 765: 		templ.interrupt_deassert = pcctwo_interrupt_deassert;
 766: 
 767: 		interrupt_handler_register(&templ);
 768: 	}
 769: 
 770: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "pcc2",
 771: 	    devinit->addr, 4096, dev_pcc2_access, (void *)d,
 772: 	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
 773: 
 774: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "mvme187_iack",
 775: 	    M187_IACK, 32, dev_mvme187_iack_access, (void *)d,
 776: 	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
 777: 
 778: 	d->timer = timer_add(PCC_TIMER_TICK_HZ, pcc_timer_tick, d);
 779: 
 780: 	machine_add_tickfunction(devinit->machine,
 781: 	    dev_pcc2_tick, d, DEV_PCC2_TICK_SHIFT);
 782: 
 783: 	return 1;
 784: }

wide rd          0x0            0x0            a guest ld.w at PRTICR (0x30..0x33, all uncased) survives an ok
wide rd srvd     1              1              ...and the announce names the width and the first served byt ok
genctl rt        0x82           0x82           an IMPLEMENTED byte register still round-trips (no C040 bit  ok
t1cmp word rt    0x12345678     0x12345678     the cased 32-bit counter arm still round-trips a word: the f ok
put b ctl        survived       survived       CONTROL: the debugger's put b never reaches a DM_DEFAULT arm ok
put b ctl msg    0              0              ...so it prints nothing -- not a witness, a control          ok
PCC2D_RESULT=30/36
PCC2D_RED=latch sameoff,latch rw one,latch rd rd,latch rd wr,latch aba,wide wr msg
PCC2D_PROBE_DONE

#!/bin/bash
#  #501 pcc2defaultkill KILL TABLE 3 -- the REPAIRED default: arm and the 36-row detector (pass-2 repair), twelve
#  mutants.  The six pass-2 mutants are scored by BOTH instruments: the 23-row one that shipped at pass 2 (frozen
#  in killtable_20260905/, md5 a96b8bcf) must stay 23/23 on them -- the measured breach -- and the 36-row one must
#  red them.  BASE owes 36/36 AND 23/23 (the old anchors survive the `(len N)' announce).
#    M_EXIT (b) verbatim exit(1) | M_UNLATCH | M_ONELATCH | M_BACKED member 6 | M_RDKILL | M_DIRSWAP   (pass 1)
#    M_WRLATCH  latch SET only on writes (grok A)        M_WIDEKILL  `len != 1' re-kill (grok B)
#    M_OFFKILL  re-kill at unsampled 0x06 (Fable)        M_SRVD0     read announce prints 0 (Opus M_O1, measured)
#    M_BACKED2  member 6 on the latched path (Opus M_O2, measured)    M_ABA  `|=' -> `=' (codex)
set -u
G=/mnt/c/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL; SEC=$G/GXEMUL-SEC; F=src/devices/dev_pcc2.c; T=/tmp/gxdk501b
OUT=/tmp/killdk501b; PROBE=pcc2_default_probe.py
OLDK=$SEC/regress/artifacts/pcc2defaultkill/killtable_20260905
say() { echo "[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] $*"; }
die() { echo "*** ABORT: $*"; exit 9; }
rm -rf "$T" "$OUT"; mkdir -p "$OUT/old23"
cp -a "$G/build" "$T" || die "clone failed"
cmp -s "$T/$F" "$SEC/$F" || { cp "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F"; say "  clone's $F refreshed from SEC (the repair; BASE rebuilds it)"; }
grep -q "(len %d)" "$T/$F" || die "the clone's $F lacks the repair"
cp "$SEC/regress/$PROBE" "$OUT/frozen_probe.py" || die "cannot freeze probe"
cp "$SEC/regress/pcc2_width_probe.py" "$OUT/pcc2_width_probe.py" || die "cannot freeze the session helper source"
cp "$OLDK/frozen_probe.py" "$OUT/old23/frozen_probe.py" || die "cannot copy the pass-2 instrument"
cp "$OLDK/pcc2_width_probe.py" "$OUT/old23/pcc2_width_probe.py" || die "cannot copy the pass-2 helper"
[ "$(md5sum "$OUT/old23/frozen_probe.py" | cut -c1-8)" = "a96b8bcf" ] || die "the pass-2 instrument is not a96b8bcf"
say "  instruments frozen: new $(md5sum "$OUT/frozen_probe.py" | cut -c1-8) ($(grep -c '^row(' "$OUT/frozen_probe.py") rows)  old23 a96b8bcf"
build() { cd "$T" || die cd; rm -f src/devices/dev_pcc2.o; make -j4 > "$OUT/build_$1.log" 2>&1; [ "$T/gxemul" -nt "$T/$F" ] || die "$1: binary not newer than source"; }
score() {   # name expect instrument-dir tag
    cd "$SEC/regress" || die cd
    python3 -u "$3/frozen_probe.py" "$T/gxemul" "$1" > "$OUT/$1$4.out" 2>&1
    grep -q PCC2D_PROBE_DONE "$OUT/$1$4.out" || die "$1$4: NO COMPLETION SENTINEL"
    say "  $1$4 [$2]: $(grep -m1 PCC2D_RESULT "$OUT/$1$4.out")"
    grep -m1 PCC2D_RED "$OUT/$1$4.out" | sed 's/PCC2D_RED=/      RED: /' || true
}
mut() {   # name expect body both|new
    echo "$F  mutant $1" > "$T/.MUTANT"; cp "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F"
    python3 - "$T/$F" "$3" <<'PYEOF'
import io, sys
p, body = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]; s = io.open(p, encoding='utf-8').read()
ns = {'s': s}; exec(body, ns); io.open(p, 'w', encoding='utf-8', newline='\n').write(ns['s'])
PYEOF
    [ $? -eq 0 ] || die "$1: mutator failed"
    cmp -s "$T/$F" "$SEC/$F" && die "$1: file UNCHANGED after mutation"
    MD=$(diff "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F" | grep -c "^[<>]"); say "    [$1] mutant diff vs SEC: $MD lines; first added: $(diff "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F" | grep "^>" | head -1 | cut -c1-100)"; [ "$MD" -gt 0 ] || die "$1: MUTATION NOT APPLIED"
    build "$1"
    say "    [$1] binary $(date -r "$T/gxemul" +%H:%M:%S)  source $(date -r "$T/$F" +%H:%M:%S)"
    score "$1" "$2" "$OUT" ""
    [ "$4" = both ] && score "$1" "pass-2 instrument: 23/23 owed = the measured breach" "$OUT/old23" ".old23"
}
say "BASE -- 36/36 owed on the repaired instrument, 23/23 on the pass-2 one"; build BASE
score BASE CONTROL "$OUT" ""; score BASE "CONTROL, pass-2 instrument" "$OUT/old23" ".old23"
[ "$(grep -m1 PCC2D_RESULT "$OUT/BASE.out" | cut -d= -f2)" = "36/36" ] || die "BASE IS NOT FULL: $(grep -m1 PCC2D_RED "$OUT/BASE.out") -- a table on a broken instrument measures nothing"

 402: /*  pcc2widthkills: decline a wide WRITE to a byte register, announcing once per
 403:     block per instance.  The caller `break's after this; the read side of the same
 404:     block never reaches here.  */
 405: static void pcc2_width_decline(struct pcc2_data *d, int bit, const char *name, int len)
 406: {
 407: 	if (!(d->warned & (1u << bit))) {
 408: 		d->warned |= (1u << bit);
 409: 		fatal("[ pcc2: unimplemented len=%i write to %s -- declined."
 410: 		    "  (once) ]\n", len, name);
 411: 	}
 412: }
 413: 
 414: DEVICE_ACCESS(pcc2)
 415: {
 416: 	uint64_t idata = 0;
 417: 	struct pcc2_data *d = (struct pcc2_data *) extra;
 418: 
 419: 	/*  0xfff42000..0xfff42fff, but only 0x40 unique registers:  */
 420: 	relative_addr %= PCC2_SIZE;
 421: 
 422: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 423: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
 424: 
 425: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
 426: 		if (relative_addr + len <= PCC2_SIZE)	/* OB-11: end-span */
 427: 			memcpy(data, d->pcctwo_reg + relative_addr, len);
 428: 		else {
 429: 			static int warned = 0;	/* #119: loud-once (course-correction) */
 430: 			if (!warned) {
 431: 				fatal("[ pcc2: read 0x%x+%i beyond reg space 0x%x; "
 432: 				    "zero-filled ]\n", (int)relative_addr, (int)len,
 433: 				    (int)PCC2_SIZE);
 434: 				warned = 1;
 435: 			}
 436: 			memset(data, 0, len);	/* OB-11b: don't leak uninit host memory */
 437: 		}

GXEMUL-SEC\src\cpus\memory_rw.c
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""pcc2widthkills DETECTOR -- the twelve width-guard blocks in DEVICE_ACCESS(pcc2).

WHAT THE FIX DID.  Twelve `if (len != 1)' blocks -- TEN `fatal(); exit(1)' kills that ended the host on
a guest-chosen access width, and two #119 warn-once latches that refused it with a
process-global static.  The
tree's own header says PCC2 registers "can be accessed as bytes, two-bytes, or
four-bytes", so the width is LEGAL.  The fix is a DIRECTION SPLIT:

    READS   ACCEPT -- the memcpy above the switch already served them, raw bytes in
            address order, width-correct by construction.  The guard discarded a
            correct answer.
    WRITES  DECLINE + per-instance latched announce -- every STORING write arm uses `idata'
            (SCCERR stores a constant 0; PSCALEADJ and IPL store nothing and print),
            the big-endian-composed value, which is "the addressed byte" ONLY at
            len==1.  Deleting the guard would store the NEIGHBOUR's lane.

CONTRACT (vacuity-floor clause (e)) -- what this detector is designed to catch:

  CATCHES
    * reinstatement of the kill at any of the SIX GUEST-reachable blocks (survival
      rows paired with a liveness observable, because exit status cannot tell
      "declined and quit" from "died with status 0");
    * *** pass 2: the two FOLDED-IN arms, IPL and MASK, had NO ROW -- twelve blocks
      converted, ten given rows, and the shipped contract did not say so.  A seat
      found it by sibling parity: MASK is GENCTL's twin and had none of GENCTL's rows.
      Repaired: IPL is EVEN and therefore the SIXTH guest-reachable block (survival +
      message rows); MASK is odd (operator rows) and gets GENCTL's readback. ***
    * reinstatement at any of the six OPERATOR-only blocks (VECBASE, PSCALEADJ, SCCICR,
      SCCRX, SCSIICR, MASK; the T1CTL/T1ICR labels are odd too but share TxCTL/TxICR's
      blocks), through the debugger's `put h' at an odd address -- MEASURED to warn
      and store and kill
      (artifacts/pcc2widthkills/operator_reach_20260903.out);
    * *** DELETE instead of decline on the write side -- the lane hazard.  `st.h
      0xAABB' to GENCTL must leave GENCTL unchanged; a bare deletion stores 0xBB,
      VECBASE's lane, because (uint8_t) idata is the HIGHEST-address byte ***;
    * an accidental per-lane compose (GENCTL would read 0xAA);
    * the value a wide READ composes -- lane order and width -- not merely survival;
    * loss of the latch in either direction: dropped `|=' floods (same site twice
      must announce ONCE), and one bit shared across blocks silences (two sites must
      announce TWICE);
    * the de-escalation clause: at the five even blocks with `acc' rows a legal byte write
      lands and reads back through the arm's own mask with NO decline message; at all
      twelve, `acc all' pins NO decline and survival (a guard grown to refuse bytes); at
      the two print-only arms the body's own print is the proof (`acc pscale ign',
      `acc ipl ro').  SCCERR's byte side is value-identical (always 0) and is disclosed,
      not claimed.

  THE ENUMERATION (pass 3 class closure, pass 4 domain repair).  Twelve blocks x NINE
  clause-(d) shapes, the helper's own two operations, and four access widths.  Two
  consecutive passes found breaches of ONE class -- a block with no channel for the
  ANNOUNCE-THEN-FALL-THROUGH shape (guard kept, announce kept, `break' removed, the
  halfword LANDS) -- at MASK, then at four odd arms, then (pass 3, MEASURED 82/82 by a
  seat) at VECBASE, whose `rb vecbase' row is GENCTL's neighbour-lane check and never
  writes wide to VECBASE's own arm.  So the space is written down, cell by cell.
  M = measured kill (log named in CHANGELOG/PRETABLE); S = same channel as a measured
  shape at that arm (the mutant lands the write exactly as the measured one does);
  E = equivalent by construction, with its warrant.  Shapes: DEL guard deleted; LEN
  `len != 1' -> `len > 2' (halfword lands, word declined); LEN2 its MIRROR, `len != 1' ->
  `len == 2' (halfword declined, WORD lands -- named by the codex seat on pass 3); KILL
  verbatim exit(1) reinstated; ALIAS one enum member for two blocks; FALL announce-then-
  fall-through; SWAP guard operand `len' -> `idata > 0xff'; GROW the guard widened to
  decline the LEGAL byte width too (`len != 1' -> `len != 0'), the de-escalation clause.

      block      DEL   LEN   LEN2  KILL  ALIAS FALL  SWAP  GROW   FALL channel
      GENCTL     M     S     W     S     C     M     M     A/C    rb genctl (table 1 M_NOBREAK)
      VECBASE    S     S     W     M     C     M     C     C      rb vecbase own  (pass-3 repair)

      C  = the cardinality row `latch all' (twelve wide writes, twelve announces): ALIAS,
           any collision drops the count; SWAP: the bare swap drops the length test and every byte preset
           kills it (part 7, 95/171); a value-test QUALIFIER on the guard is caught only
           for the VALUES rows drive AT EVERY ARM -- 3 (`latch all'), 1 (`latch all
           one') and 0 (`latch all zero') -- and, per arm, for whatever that arm's own
           wide-write rows drive (0xaabb, 0xaab8, 0x0022 ...); any other constant is not
           (passes 7-8; Opus F13).
      W  = `latch all w' (twelve WORD writes via put w, twelve announces): a `len == 2'
           guard at ANY arm lets the word land silently and drops the count.  SCCERR's
           own word rows (wrd/wwr sccerr) are its S.
      A/C = the arm's own `acc' row, AND `acc all' (twelve legal GUEST byte stores, ZERO
           declines): a guard grown to refuse bytes at ANY arm announces and reds it.
           Blocks marked C alone have no per-arm acc row; `acc all' is their channel.
           NOT the debugger's `put b': it calls memory_rw with NO_EXCEPTIONS, which does not
           dispatch to a DM_DEFAULT device, so a `put b' row could not see the grown guard
           (MEASURED: 95/96 in part 3 and 96/96 in part 3b, the mutation proven in the binary).  `put h/w/d' go
           through the store_Nbit_word helpers and do reach the arms.
      DEL/LEN/KILL cells marked S land the write, or die, exactly as the measured shape at
      that arm does, and the arm's own dcl / survival
      / readback rows are the channel.  KILL cells' channel is the survival PAIR (exit
      status plus the liveness observable), which varies under a reinstated kill by
      construction; the CHANNEL rule's 'survival-only' names a survival oracle at a
      REFUSAL-effect site, which a kill is not.  `d->warned' is a 32-bit field with twelve bits assigned.

      A NINTH SHAPE, on the input the six-shape list named and never walked: RDDECL, the
      direction split's own conjunct nulled, `writeflag == MEM_WRITE' -> `<= MEM_WRITE'
      (ONE character; MEM_READ is 0).  The Opus seat MEASURED it 82/82 on pass 3: wide
      READS were declined, announced as writes, skipped the arm's read body and consumed
      the block's latch bit -- and no session holding a wide read carried a decline-count
      row.  Closed by `hrd <block> nod' / `wrd sccerr nod' on the SEVEN wide-read sessions
      that already existed (six even blocks by ld.h, SCCERR by ld): a declined read is a
      DECL where zero is expected.  The six ODD blocks' WIDE-read cells are UNREACHABLE by
      construction -- a guest wide read at an odd offset alignment-faults before memory_rw
      (BYTE reads do reach them; see THE SECOND PREDICATE),
      and a debugger read does not dispatch to a DM_DEFAULT device -- and are disclosed,
      not rowed.  One cosmetic gap, disclosed: the seven `op * dcl' rows count the GENERIC
      decline text, and for FIVE arms (VECBASE, PSCALEADJ, SCCICR, SCCRX, SCSIICR) no other
      row pins the name string, so a change of the NAME alone there is invisible; the
      T1 labels share TxCTL/TxICR's fatal() with the named `rb t2ctl dcl' / `rb t2icr dcl'
      rows, and `latch all' pins cardinality.

      THE SHARED BLOCKS.  TxCTL serves T1CTL (0x17, odd) and T2CTL (0x16, even); TxICR
      serves T1ICR (0x1b) and T2ICR (0x1a).  The blocks' readbacks above are at the T2
      offsets; a seat noted that a fall-through reaching only the T1 labels (the block
      split in two) would leave `op t1ctl dcl' at 1 with nothing reading T1CTL back.  So
      the T1 labels carry their own operator-path readbacks, `rb t1ctl' and `rb t1icr'.
      THE THIRTEENTH ARM.  SCCRXIACK has NO width guard and never had one: its body
      ignores `idata' (it clears a bit in SCCRX and reasserts), so its effect is
      width-invariant by construction and it is not a member of the twelve.  It is named
      here so the census is not read as an omission.
      SESSIONS.  Every session() is a fresh pty.fork/execv of the emulator, so no latch
      state survives from one row to the next; `latch all' counts one process.
      THE HELPER'S OWN OPERATIONS (pass 4).  pcc2_width_decline() has two lines that
      every block depends on: the TEST `!(d->warned & (1u << bit))' and the SET
      `d->warned |= (1u << bit)'.  One-token edits to the SET line escaped every row:
      `|=' -> `=' (assign: A, B clears A -- `latch aba' A,B,A = 2) and `(1u << bit)' ->
      `1' (bit 0 always: every other block announces every time -- `latch same2', MASK
      twice = 1).  The TEST line's operand swap (`&' -> `==') is caught by `latch all':
      with the field accumulating, equality fails from the second block on and it
      announces 12 either way; `latch aba' DOES distinguish it (A=1, then the field is 3,
      and 3 == 1 is false, so A re-announces -- MEASURED 100/101 by a pass-5 seat).  An
      earlier version of this sentence called it indistinguishable; the instrument was
      stronger than its contract said.  `|=' -> `^=' or `+=' in the SET line is
      EQUIVALENT: the SET runs only under the TEST, i.e. only when the bit is clear.
      WIDTHS.  The debugger drives len 2 (`put h'), 4 (`put w') and 8 (`put d'); the
      guest drives 1, 2 and 4.  `latch all', `latch all w' and `latch all d' pin the
      ANNOUNCE at every block for each non-byte width, so a guard true on a subset of
      {2,4,8} drops one of the three counts; the REFUSAL per width is `rb *', `rb4 *'
      and `rb8 *' (see THE LANDING CHANNEL AT EVERY WIDTH).
      BIT IDENTITY.  `latch all' and `latch all w' are cardinality rows: they assert
      twelve announces, never WHICH bit.  A translation of the bit index
      (`1u << bit' -> `1u << (bit + 1)') is EQUIVALENT by construction -- no code
      outside pcc2_width_decline() reads d->warned's bits -- and was MEASURED 96/96 by a
      pass-4 seat as the clause-(f) control.  That warrant expires the day anything
      indexes or reports those bits.
      ANNOUNCE SEVERITY.  Every row counts TEXT on a pty run under -V; `fatal(' ->
      `debug(' in the helper changes no text these rows read.  NOT CAUGHT, disclosed;
      the fork's recorded warrant for fatal() over debug() is dev_asc.c's (-q makes a
      debug()-level line invisible to the boot-log hygiene grep).
      THE FAULT MEMBER.  `break' -> `return 0' at a guard turns the decline into a bus
      error.  At the six GUEST-reachable blocks the guest's store faults and the program
      never prints, so the block's `lv' row reds while its `hwr' row stays green -- the
      HOST survives (M_RET0_GENCTL, part 4: `hwr genctl lv' died, `hwr genctl' exit0; an
      earlier version of this sentence and PRETABLE_pass4 said the pair).  At
      the six OPERATOR-only blocks NOTHING above sees it -- MEASURED 101/101 at VECBASE
      by a pass-5 seat -- so `latch all fail' counts memory_rw's "failed ]" line at zero
      over the twelve-block session (M_RET0_VECBASE, part 5).
      THE FIX'S OWN STATE AS A GUARD INPUT (pass 5, MEASURED 101/101 at one arm and at all
      twelve by the Opus seat).  A guard qualified by its own latch bit -- `&& !(d->warned &
      (1u << bit))', the canonical clause-(d) conjunct -- declines and announces the FIRST
      wide write exactly as the fix does and lands the SECOND.  Every row above observed
      the device only while its latch was still armed.  Closed at every block by `rb2 *'
      (set, two wide writes, read back), `pscale noig2' and `ipl noig2' (the body's print,
      counted at zero after two writes); SCCERR is value-identical there too (E).
      THE SECOND PREDICATE.  Each arm's `if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)' is now load-bearing
      for reads that fall through to it: WIDE reads at the even blocks since the split
      (the pre-fix guard killed them first), and BYTE reads at EVERY block, always (a byte
      access skips the alignment check and passes `len != 1').  Deleting it stores
      idata == 0 on a read while the readback in the same stream -- served by the memcpy
      before the switch -- still prints the old value; only a second read sees it.
      Pinned by `hrd * rb' (wide, four even storing blocks), `hrd ipl noro', and by
      `brd2 *' (byte, read twice, all nine storing blocks) with `brd pscale noig' /

1296: > Clause (d) says *keep the guard, null its repair*. As written it enumerated only

1298: >
1299: > * a **pure deletion** introduces no predicate at all;
1300: > * a **DE-FATALISATION** introduces none either -- it changes what an EXISTING
1301: >   predicate's branch does. That is most of this project's queue.
1302: >
1303: > So for those rounds clause (d) enumerated **nothing**, and a round could satisfy the

1307: > **Reading it as DEPENDS ON fixes both cases at once**, and it is why this now replaces a
1308: > narrower deletion-only rule written earlier the same day: that rule was a special case of
1309: > this gap, and two overlapping statements of one rule is the accumulation this carrier is
1310: > supposed to refuse.
1311: >
1312: > **The two shapes, once the enumeration is right:**
1313: > * **fix ADDS a predicate** -> weaken the added one (unchanged; the original rule).
1314: > * **fix adds none** -> the (d) mutant reroutes an EXISTING guard's repair. For a
1315: >   de-fatalisation: **swap the guard's operand for a field the guarded BODY reads.**
1316: >   For a pure deletion: **re-introduce the removed behaviour under a DIFFERENT predicate**
1317: >   -- not the same one widened, which is (b) wearing (d)'s clothes.

/*	$OpenBSD: pcctworeg.h,v 1.8 2006/04/27 20:19:28 miod Exp $ */

#ifndef MVME_PCCTWOREG_H
#define MVME_PCCTWOREG_H

/*
 * Memory map for PCC2 chip found in MVME1x7 boards.
 *
 * PCCchip2 control and status register can be accessed as bytes (8 bits),
 * two-bytes (16 bits), or four-bytes (32 bits).
 */

#define	PCC2_BASE		0xfff42000
#define PCC2_SIZE		0x0040

#define	PCCTWO_CHIPID		0x0000
#define	PCCTWO_CHIPREV		0x0001
#define	PCCTWO_GENCTL		0x0002
#define	PCCTWO_VECBASE		0x0003
#define	PCCTWO_T1CMP		0x0004
#define	PCCTWO_T1COUNT		0x0008
#define	PCCTWO_T2CMP		0x000c
#define	PCCTWO_T2COUNT		0x0010
#define	PCCTWO_PSCALECNT	0x0014
#define	PCCTWO_PSCALEADJ	0x0015
#define	PCCTWO_T2CTL		0x0016
#define	PCCTWO_T1CTL		0x0017
#define	PCCTWO_GPIO_ICR		0x0018
#define	PCCTWO_GPIO_PCR		0x0019
#define	PCCTWO_T2ICR		0x001a
#define	PCCTWO_T1ICR		0x001b
#define	PCCTWO_SCCERR		0x001c
#define	PCCTWO_SCCICR		0x001d
#define	PCCTWO_SCCTX		0x001e
#define	PCCTWO_SCCRX		0x001f
#define	PCCTWO_SCCMOIACK	0x0023
#define	PCCTWO_SCCTXIACK	0x0025
#define	PCCTWO_SCCRXIACK	0x0027
#define	PCCTWO_IEERR		0x0028
#define	PCCTWO_IEICR		0x002a
#define	PCCTWO_IEBERR		0x002b
#define	PCCTWO_SCSIERR		0x002c
#define	PCCTWO_SCSIICR		0x002f
#define	PCCTWO_PRTICR		0x0030
#define	PCCTWO_PTRFICR		0x0031
#define	PCCTWO_PTRSICR		0x0032
#define	PCCTWO_PTRPICR		0x0033
#define	PCCTWO_PRTBICR		0x0034
#define	PCCTWO_PRTSTATUS	0x0036
#define	PCCTWO_PRTCTL		0x0037
#define	PCCTWO_SPEED		0x0038
#define	PCCTWO_PRTDATA		0x003a
/* The following registers are not valid on MVME197 */
#define	PCCTWO_IPL		0x003e
#define	PCCTWO_MASK		0x003f

#define PCC2_ID			0x20	/* value at CHIPID */

/* General Control Register */
#define PCC2_DR0		0x80
#define PCC2_C040		0x04
#define PCC2_MIEN		0x02
#define PCC2_FAST		0x01

/* Top 4 bits of the PCC2 VBR. Will be the top 4 bits of the vector */
#define	PCC2_VECT		0x50

/* Bottom 4 bits of the vector returned during IACK cycle */
#define PCC2V_PPBUSY		0x00 				/* lowest */
#define PCC2V_PPPE		0x01
#define PCC2V_PPSELECT		0x02
#define PCC2V_PPFAULT		0x03
#define PCC2V_PPACK		0x04
#define PCC2V_SCSI		0x05
#define PCC2V_IEFAIL		0x06
#define PCC2V_IE		0x07
#define PCC2V_TIMER2		0x08
#define PCC2V_TIMER1		0x09
#define PCC2V_GPIO		0x0a
#define PCC2V_SCC_RXE		0x0c
#define PCC2V_SCC_M		(PCC2V_SCC_RXE + 1)
#define PCC2V_SCC_TX		(PCC2V_SCC_M + 1)
#define PCC2V_SCC_RX		(PCC2V_SCC_TX + 1)

  - SIBLING PARITY -- the twelve cased byte arms DECLINE wide writes with a latched announce and ACCEPT byte writes;
    the default: arm now DECLINES byte-and-wide writes with a latched announce and keeps served reads.  Asymmetry
    disclosed: the cased arms' declines are per BLOCK, this arm's are per OFFSET (43 offsets in one arm); the
    announce says 'declined' / 'served 0x%02x' -- never 'ignored'.
  - CLAIM -> ROW -- the product comment claims: reads keep the served byte (named rd srvd, ieicr rdawr 0x0), writes
    declined (ieicr/ieerr rdawr 0x0 with sccerr rdawr as the cased control), one announce per offset per instance
    (named wr msg 18, latch sameoff 1, latch twooff 2, latch rw one 1), the fold is documented (alias 0x6a + alias msg),
    the cased arms untouched (genctl rt, t1cmp word rt), put b never reaches the arm (put b ctl).

    pcctwo_reg[relative_addr].  They disagree only in the latch's own state, which the sameoff/twooff/rw-one rows
    build.  No offset-range predicate is added (stated, not left blank).

 181:  *  the memblock split keeps doing its job unchanged.
 182:  */
 183: int MEMORY_RW(struct cpu *cpu, struct memory *mem, uint64_t vaddr,
 184: 	unsigned char *data, size_t len, int writeflag, int misc_flags)
 185: {
 186: 	if (!(misc_flags & PHYSICAL) && cpu->translate_v2p != NULL) {
 187: 		size_t granule = (size_t) MRW_SPLIT_MASK + 1;
 188: 		size_t offset = (size_t) (vaddr & MRW_SPLIT_MASK);
 189: 
 190: 		/*  `len > granule - offset`, NOT `offset + len > granule`: the sum can
 191: 		    wrap for an absurd length (offset 1, len SIZE_MAX), which would skip
 192: 		    the loop and hand the whole thing to the worker.  The subtraction
 193: 		    cannot wrap, because offset < granule always.
 194: 
 195: 		    STATED EXACTLY, because the first version of this comment implied more
 196: 		    than it delivers: this form does not SERVICE such a length, it declines
 197: 		    to mis-copy it.  A length near SIZE_MAX now iterates about 2^54 times
 198: 		    instead of silently under-splitting -- measured, a fuzz watchdog fired
 199: 		    at two million chunks.  No caller passes anything remotely like it (the
 200: 		    largest in the tree are PHYSICAL and exempt), so the case is recorded
 201: 		    and filed rather than handled.  */
 202: 		while (len > granule - offset) {
 203: 			size_t head = granule - offset;
 204: 
 205: 			if (!MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE(cpu, mem, vaddr, data, head,
 206: 			    writeflag, misc_flags)) {
 207: 				/*  #244 PROMISES THE WHOLE READ BUFFER, AND SPLITTING BROKE
 208: 				    THAT: the worker zeroes only the chunk IT was given, so a
 209: 				    failure before the last chunk used to leave the caller's
 210: 				    remaining bytes untouched -- uninitialised host stack, for
 211: 				    a caller that ignores the return value.  Unsplit, one call
 212: 				    covered the whole length.  Zero the rest here so the
 213: 				    guarantee is the same as it was.  */
 214: 				if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 215: 					memset(data, 0, len);
 216: 				return MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED;
 217: 			}
 218: 
 219: 			vaddr += head;
 220: 			data += head;
 221: 			len -= head;
 222: 			offset = 0;
 223: 		}
 224: 	}
 225: 
 226: 	return MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE(cpu, mem, vaddr, data, len, writeflag, misc_flags);
 227: }

 580: 						    orig_paddr & ~offset_mask);
 581: 				}
 582: 
 583: 				res = 0;
 584: 				if (!no_exceptions || (mem->devices[i].flags &
 585: 				    DM_READS_HAVE_NO_SIDE_EFFECTS)) {
 586: 					bool running_before_device_access = cpu->running;
 587: 					res = mem->devices[i].f(cpu, mem, paddr,
 588: 					    data, len, writeflag,
 589: 					    mem->devices[i].extra);
 590: 
 591: 					if (running_before_device_access && !cpu->running)
 592: 						return MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED;
 593: 				}
 594: 
 595: 				if (res == 0)
 596: 					res = -1;
 597: 
 598: 				/*
 599: 				 *  If accessing the memory mapped device
 600: 				 *  failed, then return with an exception.
 601: 				 *  (Architecture specific.)
 602: 				 */
 603: 				if (res <= 0 && !no_exceptions) {
 604: 					debug("[ %s device '%s' addr %08lx "
 605: 					    "failed ]\n", writeflag?
 606: 					    "writing to" : "reading from",
 607: 					    mem->devices[i].name, (long)paddr);
 608: #ifdef MEM_MIPS
 609: 					mips_cpu_exception(cpu,
 610: 					    cache == CACHE_INSTRUCTION?
 611: 					    EXCEPTION_IBE : EXCEPTION_DBE,
 612: 					    0, vaddr, 0, 0, 0, 0);
 613: #endif
 614: #ifdef MEM_M88K
 615: 					/*  TODO: This is enough for
 616: 					    OpenBSD/mvme88k's badaddr()
 617: 					    implementation... but the
 618: 					    faulting address should probably
 619: 					    be included somewhere too!  */
 620: 					m88k_exception(cpu, cache == CACHE_INSTRUCTION
 621: 					    ? M88K_EXCEPTION_INSTRUCTION_ACCESS
 622: 					    : M88K_EXCEPTION_DATA_ACCESS, 0);
 468:     print("%-16s %-12s %-12s %-46s %s" % (name, str(got), str(want), note,
 469:                                           "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
 470:     return ok
 471: 
 472: 
 473: def session(cmds, settle=1.5, spin=1.2):
 474:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
 475:     if pid == 0:
 476:         os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "mvme88k", "-e", "mvme187", "-M", "64",
 477:                        "0x10000:" + DUMMY])
 478:         os._exit(127)
 479:     buf = ""
 480: 
 481:     def rd(t, until=None):
 482:         #  drain the pty for t seconds; with `until', return as soon as that text has
 483:         #  arrived (pass 7: a fixed drain after `continue' could send the next command to a
 484:         #  guest that had not reached its breakpoint yet).
 485:         nonlocal buf
 486:         end = time.time() + t
 487:         while time.time() < end:
 488:             if until is not None and until in buf:
 489:                 return True
 490:             r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
 491:             if not r:
 492:                 continue
 493:             try:
 494:                 d = os.read(fd, 65536)
 495:                 if not d:
 496:                     return False
 497:                 buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
 498:             except OSError:
 499:                 return False
 500:         if until is not None and until in buf:    # pass 9 (codex): the token may land in the last read
 501:             return True
 502:         return "timeout" if until is not None else True
 503: 
 504:     alive = rd(settle)
 505:     how = "nostatus"
 506:     for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
 507:         w = spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.25
 508:         until = None
 509:         if isinstance(c, tuple):    # (cmd, wait[, until]): `continue' waits for its breakpoint
 510:             until = c[2] if len(c) > 2 else None    # read the tuple BEFORE unpacking it
 511:             c, w = c[0], c[1]
 512:         try:
 513:             #  the one unbounded call left (pass 9, Opus): it blocks only if the pty input buffer fills
 514:             #  while the guest free-runs -- tens of bytes per command against a kilobyte buffer.
 515:             os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
 516:         except OSError:
 517:             alive = False
 518:             break
 519:         r = rd(w, until)
 520:         if r is False:
 521:             alive = False
 522:             break
 523:         if r == "timeout":
 524:             #  pass 8 (codex, grok): the guest never reached its breakpoint -- send it nothing
 525:             #  more, and let every row of this session say so through cval().
 526:             how = "no-breakpoint"
 527:             break
 528:     try:
 529:         os.write(fd, b"quit\n")
 530:         rd(0.4)
 531:     except OSError:
 532:         pass
 533:     try:
 534:         os.close(fd)
 535:     except OSError:
 536:         pass
 537:     how = how if how == "no-breakpoint" else "nostatus"
 538:     #  pass 7: a guest still running (no breakpoint reached) would block waitpid forever;
 539:     #  give it a moment after the pty closed, then kill it -- the row says so, the gate
 540:     #  does not hang.
 541:     stat = None
 542:     reaped = False
 543:     for _ in range(30):
 544:         try:
 545:             p, stat = os.waitpid(pid, os.WNOHANG)
 546:         except OSError:
 547:             p, stat = pid, None
 548:         if p:
 549:             reaped = True
 550:             break
 551:         time.sleep(0.1)
 552:     if not reaped:
 553:         try:
 554:             os.kill(pid, 9)
 555:         except OSError:
 556:             pass
 557:         how = how if how == "no-breakpoint" else "killed"
 558:         for _ in range(30):    # pass 8 (Opus F2): reap the killed child, bounded
 559:             try:
 560:                 p, stat = os.waitpid(pid, os.WNOHANG)
 561:             except OSError:
 562:                 p, stat = pid, None
 563:             if p:
 564:                 reaped = True
 565:                 break
 566:             time.sleep(0.1)
 567:         if not reaped:
 568:             how = how if how == "no-breakpoint" else "unreaped"
 569:     try:
 570:         if stat is None or not reaped or how in ("no-breakpoint", "unreaped", "killed"):
 571:             pass
 572:         elif os.WIFSIGNALED(stat):
 573:             how = "sig%d" % os.WTERMSIG(stat)
 574:         elif os.WIFEXITED(stat):
 575:             how = "exit%d" % os.WEXITSTATUS(stat)
 576:     except OSError:
 577:         pass
 578:     return buf.replace("\r", ""), alive, how
 579: 
 580: 
 581: def printed(t, reg):
 582:     m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
 583:     return "0x%x" % int(m.group(1), 16) if m else "absent"

   1: # Round #501 pcc2defaultkill -- the `default:` arm of DEVICE_ACCESS(pcc2) ended the host on any access to an uncased offset, reads included; now reads keep the served byte and writes are declined, one latched announce per offset
   2: 
   3: **2026-09-05.  HIGH, ranked first by the 2026-09-04 fix-review adjudication.  Product change: `src/devices/dev_pcc2.c`
   4: -- the `exit(1)` replaced by a per-offset latched `fatal()` (`uint64_t warned_unimpl`, one bit per byte offset) that
   5: says DECLINED for a write and SERVED 0x%02x for a read, then `break` to the handler's single `return 1`.  Detector:
   6: `regress/pcc2_default_probe.py`, 23 rows, run by gate 14.  Witness: rung 3, measured before the edit.**
   7: 
   8: ## The defect and its witness
   9: 
  10: Forty-three of the 0x40 byte offsets have no `case` (derived from `mvme_pcctworeg.h` against the 21 labels; two seats
  11: re-derived it): eighteen NAMED registers the model does not implement (PSCALECNT, GPIO_ICR/PCR, SCCMOIACK, SCCTXIACK,
  12: IEERR, IEICR, IEBERR, SCSIERR, the printer block, SPEED, PRTDATA), the twelve bytes INSIDE the four 32-bit
  13: counter/compare words, and thirteen unnamed offsets.  Any access to one of them -- a READ included, although the
  14: memcpy above the switch had already served it -- reached `default:` and `exit(1)`.
  15: 
  16: **The witness (`regress/artifacts/pcc2defaultkill/witness_20260905/`), measured on the committed build BEFORE the
  17: edit: 7/13.**  A guest `st.b` to IEICR (0x2a) ends the host with exit status 1, the arm's own `pcc2: unimplemented`
  18: line printed first (the arm was reached); a guest `ld.bu` there likewise -- the READ side kills too; IEBERR and the
  19: in-word byte 0x05 and the alias 0x6a kill the same way.  Liveness (the probe's scratch round-trip) and
  20: device-signature (CHIPID reads PCC2_ID 0x20) controls pass.  **The debugger's `put b` does NOT reach the arm** -- it
  21: survives and prints nothing -- which the filing had called the cheapest witness (measured false; corrected).  The
  22: witness script is the width probe's own `session()` helper: a first draft hand-rolled the pty wait, accepted a
  23: STALE prompt, and read every kill as "survived" -- the carrier's rule about hand-rolled pty plumbing, re-learned.
  24: The combined IEBERR/IEERR row measures IEBERR only (the exiting build dies at the first); IEERR's kill follows from
  25: source (codex).  **13/13 on the fixed build.**

  27: ## The design (pass 1: nine seats, `_scratchpad/panel_20260905_021751`, converged)
  28: 
  29: READ: keep the byte the memcpy served and SAY it is served -- it is the register array's default, not a computed
  30: value, so this is not the option set's member 4 and a bounded announce about it is not a wrong record.  WRITE:
  31: member 3, DECLINE + latched announce -- NOT member 6 (backed accept), on three warrants the seats measured or read:
  32: the file's own cased siblings contradict a blanket store (SCCERR is write-to-clear -- the board manual's SCLR bits
  33: read as zero; the ICRs mask and reassert; PSCALECNT/SPEED/the IACKs are read-only); the addition licence is unmet
  34: (no guest reaches these offsets on this model); a store from the one arm 43 offsets reach adds a bounded-write
  35: surface into `pcctwo_reg[]` at 0x3d.  The Opus seat BUILT both candidates in a scratch clone and measured the
  36: 13-row witness at 13/13 under BOTH -- disposition-blind -- so the detector carries read-after-write rows in ONE
  37: session (`ieicr rdawr`, `ieerr rdawr`: store 0x5a then load, want 0x0 -- a backed accept reads 0x5a) with the CASED
  38: write-to-clear sibling `sccerr rdawr` as the control that reads 0x0 under either.
  39: 
  40: **Two premises of the pass-1 brief were wrong and are corrected here.**  The in-tree guest driver (`if_ie.c`) writes
  41: three of these offsets, but NEVER RUNS on this model: `machine_mvme88k.c:106` installs `unreadable` at the 82596
  42: address, `if_ie.c:407-409` fails its match on `badpaddr()` there, and `ie_setupram()` returns early at `:561-564`
  43: before the PCC2 store at `:609` (Fable, Opus, grok, codex independently; verified).  `ie0` IS configured in
  44: `conf/M187` and `conf/NFS` -- the brief's conf grep looked in the wrong files.  So the HIGH ranking rests on the
  45: witness and severity, not on the driver.  And the alias the model implements (`relative_addr %= PCC2_SIZE`) is the
  46: REAL part's: Motorola VMESBCP2 Table 6-1 lists `$FFF42040-$FFF42FFF  PCCchip2 Registers  Repeated` -- the codex seat
  47: cited it, the manual was fetched into `_scratchpad/` and the line read (`vmesbcp2.txt:4629`).  Two seats had rightly
  48: called the fold unestablished before the manual was on disk.
  49: 
  50: Recorded alternatives: the codex seat's ONE latch for the whole site, writes only (not adopted: a driver scanning
  51: registers should see each distinct one announced; both forms are bounded).  Corrections to the option set from this
  52: round: `return 0` denotes a FAILED TRANSACTION, not inherently unmapped hardware (an IACK cycle with no interrupt
  53: pending times out on the real part).  Disclosed: the in-word bytes are the interior of IMPLEMENTED counters, declined
  54: here as a width/alignment matter (`pcc2widecompose`), not claimed as unimplemented registers; the IACK offsets
  55: 0x23/0x25 are declined like the rest -- modelling them on the SCCRXIACK shape would open two new entries into the
  56: C040 path, so `pcc2c040kill` goes first (filed as a cross-round obligation); per-instance latch scope is not
  57: provable by two fresh sessions (each is a new process) -- a two-instance fixture is filed.

 106: ## Pass 2 (nine seats on the real diff, `_scratchpad/panel_20260905_055656`): the 23-row detector was BELOW THE FLOOR, six ways
 107: 
 108: **Roster.**  codex (GPT-6 Astra, max), grok, kimi (the CLI stalled on the 54 KB pointer form -- 4.5 KB of paging
 109: monologue, no answer, `kimi.stall_pointerform.txt` -- and was RE-FIRED ONCE with the 19 KB packet inline; it then
 110: answered), glm and deepseek (thinking-burned on the full packet -- `ollama_*.burn.txt` -- and answered 2 KB
 111: one-question mini packets), minimax, Opus 5 and Fable 5 (Agent seats; answers preserved as `opus5.txt`, `fable5.txt`).
 112: agy DELIBERATELY NOT FIRED (measurement reserve).  Five seats said PASS or GO (kimi, minimax, glm, deepseek; Fable
 113: GO-WITH-CHANGES, provisional); three said NOT CLOSE (codex, grok, Opus).  **Count the arguments: the three carried
 114: measurements or predictions the five had not tested, and the Opus seat's two were MEASURED in its own scratch clone
 115: against the frozen instrument pair (md5-verified equal to the live one).**  The fix itself every seat held right:
 116: the fold bounds the shift (`uint64_t`, `%= 0x40`), `break` reaches the one `return 1`, the latch words are separate
 117: fields both zeroed at DEVINIT, no cased arm is touched -- and, as Opus put it, the arm can only add output where the
 118: old code called `exit(1)`, so no boot that works today can regress.
 119: 
 120: **The six escapes of the 23-row instrument** (every one a clause-(d)-shaped edit of the new arm; the first two
 121: MEASURED at 23/23, the rest predicted from the row list and measured in table 3 below):
 122: 
 123: | seat | mutant | why 23 rows could not see it |
 124: |---|---|---|
 125: | Opus (MEASURED 23/23) | the read announce's value `-> 0` (one token) | `named rd srvd` counts the PHRASE `served 0x`, never the value; no row served a nonzero byte |
 126: | Opus (MEASURED 23/23) | member 6 on the LATCHED path: `} else if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) pcctwo_reg[ra] = idata;` | both `rdawr` rows store ONCE to a fresh offset in a fresh process; the latch state was built by `sameoff`/`twooff`/`rw one` and observed on the COUNT channel only |
 127: | grok | the latch SET conditioned on writes | the bit-set cell was reached only through a WRITE (`rw one`); two reads at one offset had no row -- the setter's direction is a third axis the (writeflag x latch) 2x2 hides |
 128: | grok | `if (len != 1) exit(1);` -- the removed kill was width-agnostic, so this is a PARTIAL reinstatement | every default-path row was `st.b`/`ld.bu`; the pass-1 PRETABLE's own SIBLING PARITY sentence said "byte-and-wide" and no row drove wide |
 129: | codex | `|=` `->` `=` at the latch update (forgets earlier bits when a new offset announces) | A,A and A,B and W,R never REVISIT an offset after another has announced; A,B,A wants 2 and the mutant prints 3 |
 130: | Fable | `if (relative_addr == 0x06) exit(1);` -- a re-kill at an offset the rows never drove | writes drove 27 of the 43 offsets and reads 18 (codex's count); the pass-1 contract had DISCLOSED the sampling |
 131: 
 132: **The diagnosis, from the pass-1 PRETABLE's own GUARD-FIELD line:** *"they disagree only in the latch's own state,
 133: which the sameoff/twooff/rw-one rows build"* -- true, and the state was observed on one channel (the announce count)
 134: while the mutant that matters (Opus M_O2) changes the OTHER channel (the read-back value) in exactly that state.  The
 135: guard/body check found the cell and the row was written against the wrong observable.  And codex found a product
 136: defect the rows could not: the read announce RELOADED `pcctwo_reg[relative_addr]` after the response was copied, and
 137: the SIGALRM tick rewrites the counter interiors -- so for the twelve in-word offsets the announced byte could differ
 138: from the served one.  `"the array's default, NOT a computed value"` was therefore also wrong for those twelve.
 139: 
 140: **The repair (one diff, class closure -- the repair-chain amendment: consecutive cells of one class are closed
 141: together, not one per pass).**  PRODUCT: both announces carry `(len %d)`; the read announce prints `data[0]` -- the
 142: byte actually SERVED -- with a comment saying why.  DETECTOR, 36 rows (+13): `latch rd rd` and `latch rd wr` complete
 143: the setter x tester product (W,W `sameoff`; W,R `rw one`; R,R; R,W); `latch aba` pins accumulation across offsets;
 144: `sameoff val` reads back after the SECOND write (the latched path's disposition); `srvd val` + `srvd val text` serve
 145: a byte the GUEST wrote (a word store through the cased T1CMP arm puts 0x34 at 0x05) and pin the announced value;
 146: `wide wr` (+`msg`) and `wide rd` (+`srvd`) drive `st.w`/`ld.w` at uncased word-aligned offsets and pin the width
 147: text; `inword wr`/`unnamed wr` now drive all 12 / all 13, and `inword rd`/`unnamed rd` read them -- **every one of
 148: the 43 uncased offsets is written once and read once**; `wr declined` pins the word.  Row names stay under 14
 149: characters (the gate's `%-16s` anchor).  RECORDS (the L4s the seats named): the kill-table header's `c76e053a ==
 150: live` (run 1's md5 against run 2's table; the live probe was `a96b8bcf`); "three predictions exact" (four); the
 151: M_EXIT diagnosis (its 16-row red SET was right, only the numeral and the derived score were wrong); "would have

env = {'setup': lambda **kw: [], 'program':lambda insns:insns, 'I_ST_SCR':'control-store','I_LD_SCR':'control-load'}
for node in probe.body:
    if isinstance(node,ast.Assign):
        try:
            value = ast.literal_eval(node.value)
        except (ValueError,TypeError):
            continue
        target = node.targets[0]
        if isinstance(target,ast.Tuple):
            env.update((t.id,v) for t,v in zip(target.elts,value))

        args=eval(compile(ast.Expression(node.value.args[0]),'<static session census>','eval'), {'__builtins__':{}},env)
        sessions.append({'events':[e for e in args if isinstance(e,tuple)],'rows':[]})
    if isinstance(node,ast.Expr) and isinstance(node.value,ast.Call) and isinstance(node.value.func,ast.Name) and node.value.func.id=='row':

 809: 		cmd_len = strlen(cmd);
 810:

 812: 		while (cmd_len > 0 && cmd[0]==' ')
 813: 			memmove(cmd, cmd+1, cmd_len --);
 814: 		while (cmd_len > 0 && cmd[cmd_len-1] == ' ')
 815: 			cmd[(cmd_len--)-1] = '\0';
 816: 
 817: 		/*  No command? Then try reading another line.  */
 818: 		if (cmd_len == 0) {
 819: 			/*  Special case for repeated commands:  */
 820: 			if (repeat_cmd[0] != '\0')
 821: 				strlcpy(cmd, repeat_cmd, MAX_CMD_BUFLEN);
 822: 			else
 823: 				continue;
 824: 		} else {
 825: 			last_cmd_index ++;
 826: 			if (last_cmd_index >= N_PREVIOUS_CMDS)
 827: 				last_cmd_index = 0;
 828: 
 829: 			repeat_cmd[0] = '\0';
 830: 		}
 831:

 835: 		if (exit_debugger_to_continue_single_stepping)
 836: 			return;
 837: 	}

I read all 928 lines and checked the specified working tree, frozen probe hashes, source diff, predictions, and individual result files. New escapes below are **derived predictions, not measured sweeps**; this workspace is read-only.

1. **The product repair is sound, but its race rationale overcounts the affected bytes.**

   For valid, nonempty reads, `data[0]` contains the copied byte or the end-span zero fill, and nothing after the announcement changes it. Writes announce `idata`. Supported access lengths fit `int`; the existing text anchors survive, corroborated by BASE’s old-instrument results. [Handler](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_pcc2.c:414)

   However, the timer writes back **T1COUNT and T2COUNT only**. Their six interior bytes can change asynchronously; the six CMP interiors are not timer-written. F2.7 and the repeated “twelve interiors” rationale need correction. This matters directly: the new nonzero-value fixture uses **T1CMP**, outside the changing subset. [Timer writebacks](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_pcc2.c:259)

2. **All twelve measured mutants die through their claimed channels. One predicted observable is wrong.**

   The individual outputs agree with all twelve reported scores and red sets. All six `.old23` escape results are 23/23. I found no mutant whose claimed killing row stayed green while an unrelated row rescued the result. [Recorded run](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/pcc2defaultkill/killtable3_20260905/driver.log)

   But PRETABLE predicts `M_UNLATCH / latch sameoff = 2`; the measurement is **3**. The appended read also announces when the latch guard is disabled. The score and red set remain exact, so this is an observable-count miss, not an unsafe predicted-red-to-green miss. Record it without rewriting the original prediction. The contract and row explanation also retain the incorrect “2” claim. [Measured row](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/pcc2defaultkill/killtable3_20260905/M_UNLATCH.out:17)

3. **The closure still lacks a value observable for reads with the latch set.**

   Name this mutant `M_LATCH_RD_ZERO`: extend the existing latch conditional with an `else if (writeflag == MEM_READ)` that sets `data[0] = 0`.

   **Prediction: 36/36.** `latch rd rd` checks announcement count only. Every checked read after a preceding access—`ieicr rdawr`, `ieerr rdawr`, `sameoff val`, and `wide wr`—expects zero already. `srvd val` reads its nonzero byte with the latch initially clear, so it remains green. [Affected fixtures](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/pcc2_default_probe.py:138)

   This repeats the accepted M_BACKED2 defect on the **read response** channel. It uses the existing latch-state and direction partitions; it does not require an invented offset or value predicate. Under the packet’s own floor and channel reasoning, this is an open floor cell.

   Close it with nonzero preservation checks after both read-set and write-set latches. Seeding a CMP interior through its cased word arm provides stable backing data. Count-only coverage of the four direction sequences does not establish their value behavior.

   Len-2-only behavior and instance scope remain disclosed exclusions under the stated partition bound. Neither exclusion excuses this single-instance read-value gap.

4. **The smallest escapes include edits inside the repair itself.**

   Three further predictions are concrete:

   | Mutant | Why the 36 rows are predicted to remain green |
   |---|---|
   | Restore the read argument from `data[0]` to `d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr]`, retaining the width label | `srvd val text` reads stable CMP data; interior coverage checks survival. Every detector program uses debugger stepping, which leaves timers stopped. |
   | Replace `len` with `4` in either new announcement argument | The width assertions require only `(len 4)`. Byte-access rows never require `(len 1)`, so byte accesses can be mislabeled. |
   | Replace the write announcement’s `idata` with `0` | Write rows check direction, decline wording, offset, width, or count—not the announced write value. |

   The latter two are one-token substitutions. The first directly reverses the substantive read-announcement repair. [Announcement arguments](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_pcc2.c:690), [step bypasses timer restart](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/debugger/debugger.c:834)

   The missing measurement for the first is a deterministic fixture that changes backing data **between response copying and announcement**, then checks announcement against response. Merely running a timer does not guarantee that interval is exercised.

   Another exclusion needs naming: replacing `fatal` with `debug` can preserve these stepping-session outputs while suppressing diagnostics during quiet execution. The width detector already discloses that limitation.

5. **The record contains unresolved contradictions despite F2.9 saying they were corrected in place.**

   The live CHANGELOG still says “the register array’s default” at line 29, “no guest reaches” at line 34, and “would have shipped green and counted nothing” at line 97. Its M_EXIT account still says the prediction *listed fifteen* rows, although the preserved prediction enumerates sixteen and mislabels their count. Later correction paragraphs contradict these retained statements. Its opening also still advertises 23 detector rows. [CHANGELOG](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/CHANGELOG.md:29)

   Gate 14’s own denominator is **857 checks**. I reproduced 875 only by including the 18 nested PIT-probe `ok` lines. Label those quantities separately. The PCC2 counts and `GATE_RC=0` are supported. [Gate result](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/pcc2defaultkill/killtable3_20260905/gate14_501b.log:1322)

   F2.10’s safety sentence has a narrow warrant: previously successful execution traces in the original exiting implementation did not enter this arm. It does not establish safety against the already-surviving pass-2 implementation or constitute boot validation.

   The roster is nine configured seats, eight responding, with agy explicitly withheld. The saved `SEATS.txt` still reports kimi missing despite the preserved refire answer; that roster artifact needs reconciliation.

6. **Do not close the composite on the current zero.**

   The defensible measured statement is **0 survivors out of these 12 shapes**: `M_WRLATCH`, `M_WIDEKILL`, `M_OFFKILL`, `M_SRVD0`, `M_BACKED2`, `M_ABA`, `M_EXIT`, `M_UNLATCH`, `M_ONELATCH`, `M_BACKED`, `M_RDKILL`, and `M_DIRSWAP`.

   That denominator excludes the newly derived escapes above, plus len-2-only behavior, end-span reads, and instance scope. The 43-offset census is correct; driving every offset in both directions establishes access coverage, not value and announcement correctness in every latch state.

   Closure requires measuring the named escapes, adding discriminating observables, and correcting the record. The existing twelve kills do not discharge those obligations.
